Um, yeah, I guess you could say that. I really try to just stay put, I promise. It's not a scary feeling at all, it's just... more like my body has too much energy and I just need to let a little bit out so I can focus better. I'm definitely not an anxious person, I promise. I hope that's the right answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention to tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I really do try my absolute best to focus on my work, I promise. But, I guess... well, sometimes when I have to do the inventory logs, or if I'm supposed to be reading the CNC training pages? I always start out really trying to pay attention. But then, after just a few minutes, it's like the numbers or the words just start to blur together. \n\nI'll get to the bottom of the page and realize I didn't take any of it in at all, so I have to keep going back and starting over. I don't just... do things without thinking about them first. So I feel like I absolutely have to move, or pace a little, or stretch, or just go do something right then and there. I try really hard to fight it, though! I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad employee, I just get so uncomfortable. Does that make sense, how I explained it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when this pressure builds up inside your body?\n\nPatient: No, no, I wouldn't say I feel anxious. I'm actually a pretty happy person, really! I don't get nervous or worried about things like that. It's not a scary feeling at all, it's just... more like my body has too much energy and I just need to let a little bit out so I can focus better. I'm definitely not an anxious person, I promise. I hope that's the right answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention to tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I really do try my absolute best to focus on my work, I promise. But, I guess... well, sometimes when I have to do the inventory logs, or if I'm supposed to be reading the CNC training pages? I always start out really trying to pay attention. But then, after just a few minutes, it's like the numbers or the words just start to blur together. \n\nI'll get to the bottom of the page and realize I didn't take any of it in at all, so I have to keep going back and starting over. I always make sure to get it done, though! I just have to re-read things a lot to make it stick. I really hope that doesn't make me sound lazy or, um, like I'm not taking the trainings seriously. I really do want to do a good job. Is it okay that I struggle with that part a little bit?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't really think so. I mean, I try my absolute best to be careful, especially out on the production floor with all the equipment. Safety is really important to me, I promise! I don't just... do things without thinking about them first. I always want to make sure I'm following the supervisor's rules and doing exactly what I'm supposed to be doing. \n\nI really hope that's the right answer? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm reckless or a troublemaker or anything like that. I try to be a really good worker. Am I understanding your question okay?\n\nDoctor: When did you first start noticing that you had trouble sitting still or focusing on tasks?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well... I guess it's actually been a really long time. Like, since I was little. I hope you don't judge me for this, because I really did try to be a good student, I promise! But... even way back in elementary school, I guess I was sort of that kid who just couldn't quite stay in her seat. \n\nI didn't mean to be disruptive or anything! I would just get so eager or excited that I'd accidentally talk over the teacher, or I'd get notes sent home to my parents because I'd blurt things out or just kind of... wander around the room when we were supposed to be doing our desk work. I always felt so awful about those notes because I really never wanted to cause trouble. I just had so much energy and it was hard to keep it all in, you know? \n\nIs it normal that it started that far back? I hope I'm explaining this okay... I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had trouble keeping a job or getting along with friends because of your difficulty sitting still or focusing?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess, maybe a little bit? I really try to be the best worker I can be, I really do! But... um, well, I have gotten a few write-ups at work. I felt so bad about them! They were for, um, safety and production things. I didn't mean to mess up, I just get a little ahead of myself sometimes, or I miss a detail when I'm trying to work fast. I'm really worried it might affect my plans to get certified, which I want so badly. \n\nAnd, well, I guess some of my coworkers... they don't always want to partner with me during handoffs. I think maybe I just frustrate them because I'm always moving around or talking a lot, and maybe I miss things they say. I really try to be a good team player, though! I always apologize if I mess up. Do you think they hate working with me? I just want everyone to like me and think I'm doing a good job. Is it okay that I'm struggling with this?\n\nDoctor: Do you have mood swings or get easily upset?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I try really hard to keep a positive attitude, especially at work. I know nobody likes being around someone who's always grumpy or upset, so I really try to just be cheerful and easy to get along with. \n\nI guess sometimes I might get a little frustrated if I'm trying to do something and it's not working out right, but I don't... I wouldn't call it mood swings. I don't fly off the handle or anything like that, I promise! I just want to be a pleasant person to be around. Is that what you mean? I hope I'm answering that right. I really try not to let things get to me too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your trouble with focusing on tasks is more of a problem for you than your tendency to move around or fidget?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... um, I'm not really sure how to compare them, to be honest. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... yeah, I guess it does kind of bleed into everything. I try so hard to keep it all together, especially at work, because I really want to do a good job and be helpful. But... um... I guess it has been a little bit of a problem there lately. 

I don't want you to think I'm a bad worker! I really do try my best. But, well, my supervisors have had to talk to me a couple of times... they actually wrote me up, just for some safety and production things. I guess I just get a little too rushed or distracted sometimes. And I'm really worried because I think it might be putting my job and my plans to get my certifications at risk. 

Plus, I think some of my coworkers are getting a little frustrated with me. Like, they seem less willing to partner up with me during handoffs, and I hate that because I just want everyone to get along and be a good team. I really am trying to fix it, I promise! Does that make sense? I just hope my boss knows I'm not doing it on purpose... I really want to be a good employee.

Um... outside of work? Well, I guess... I mean, I try really, really hard to sit still, especially when I'm supposed to be paying attention. I don't want anyone to think I'm not listening! But, um... if I'm in my certification class, or like, if we have a long safety meeting... I guess I do move around a bit. 

I don't mean to be a distraction, I promise! But my foot just kind of starts bouncing on its own, or I'll realize I'm clicking my pen a bunch of times, or just... picking at my sleeves. I try to stop when I notice it, but sometimes I'm just shifting in my seat so much that the people next to me will actually ask me to stop. It's so embarrassing when that happens, because I really just want to be respectful and pay attention to whoever is talking. Do you think it's okay that I just have a little trouble staying totally frozen in one spot? I really am trying to be better about it!

I would just get so eager or excited that I'd accidentally talk over the teacher, or I'd get notes sent home to my parents because I'd blurt things out or just kind of... wander around the room when we were supposed to be doing our desk work. I always felt so awful about those notes because I really never wanted to cause trouble. I just had so much energy and it was hard to keep it all in, you know? \n\nIs it normal that it started that far back? I hope I'm explaining this okay... I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had trouble keeping a job or getting along with friends because of your difficulty sitting still or focusing?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess, maybe a little bit? I really try to be the best worker I can be, I really do! But... um, well, I have gotten a few write-ups at work. I felt so bad about them! They were for, um, safety and production things. I didn't mean to mess up, I just get a little ahead of myself sometimes, or I miss a detail when I'm trying to work fast. I'm really worried it might affect my plans to get certified, which I want so badly. \n\nAnd, well, I guess some of my coworkers... they don't always want to partner with me during handoffs. I think maybe I just frustrate them because I'm always moving around or talking a lot, and maybe I miss things they say. I really try to be a good team player, though! I always apologize if I mess up. Do you think they hate working with me? I just want everyone to like me and think I'm doing a good job. Is it okay that I'm struggling with this?\n\nDoctor: Do you have mood swings or get easily upset?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I try really hard to keep a positive attitude, especially at work. I know nobody likes being around someone who's always grumpy or upset, so I really try to just be cheerful and easy to get along with. \n\nI guess sometimes I might get a little frustrated if I'm trying to do something and it's not working out right, but I don't... I wouldn't call it mood swings. I don't fly off the handle or anything like that, I promise! I just want to be a pleasant person to be around. Is that what you mean? I hope I'm answering that right. I really try not to let things get to me too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your trouble with focusing on tasks is more of a problem for you than your tendency to move around or fidget?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... um, I'm not really sure how to compare them, to be honest. I guess I don't really know which one is... worse? I mean, I really try my best not to let either of them be a bother to anyone! I know I'm always kind of moving around or tapping my feet, and yeah, maybe my mind wanders sometimes when I'm supposed to be focusing, but I really do try so hard to stay on track. \n\nI don't want to say one is a bigger problem than the other because... well, I'm hoping neither of them is too big of a deal, if that makes sense? I just want to do a good job at work and be helpful. Is it okay if I don't really have a clear answer for which one is more of a problem? I hope that's alright... I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble sitting still or focusing in situations outside of work or school?\n\nPatient: Um... outside of work? I don't think it's a huge problem? \n\nLike, if I go to the movies with my friends or something, I might get up to get snacks a few times, or I might kind of bounce my leg, but I always try to make sure I'm not bothering anyone else in the theater! I really don't want to be annoying. And at home, I just like to keep busy, you know? Cleaning up or organizing things. I guess I don't sit down to watch a whole TV show very often, but that's just because I like being productive! \n\nI really try to be respectful in public places, like waiting rooms or whatever. I might pace a little bit if I'm waiting a long time, but I try to stay out of the way. Do you think that sounds okay? I hope I'm not doing anything too weird when I'm out and about. I just want to blend in and be polite, you know?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these difficulties with sitting still and focusing consistently throughout your life?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess it's been happening for a really long time. Um... oh gosh. I really, really try not to do that, because I know it's not polite. But... I guess sometimes I do kind of cut in on people without meaning to. 

I don't mean to be rude or anything, I promise! It's just... like, if my coworkers are having a conversation, I might just sort of jump right in before they're finished. Or, um, if the shift lead is asking a question, I'll catch myself blurting out the answer before they're even done talking. 

I just get so eager to help or join in, but then... well, everybody gets a little irritated with me. And I hate that, because I really want them to like me and I want to be respectful. It's just like the words come out before I can stop them, you know? Do you think people understand that I'm not trying to be disrespectful? I really am trying so hard to get better at waiting my turn to speak...

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... oh, well... I guess it depends on what it is? If it's something I really, really love doing, I can usually stay with it a little better. But... um... if it's something I have to do for work, even if I know it's super important, I still kind of struggle. 

Like, when I'm doing the inventory logs or... or reading the CNC training pages? I promise I start out really trying my best to pay attention! I sit down and I'm like, "Okay, I'm going to read this and understand it." But then... well, after just a few minutes, it's like the numbers or the words just start to blur together. I end up having to go back and read the same thing over and over because I realize I didn't actually take any of it in. 

I don't mean to be careless with it, I really don't! It's just so hard to keep my brain locked onto it. Does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm just not trying hard enough, because I really am giving it my all...

Is it okay that I'm struggling with this?\n\nDoctor: Do you have mood swings or get easily upset?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I just really hope they knew I wasn't trying to be disrespectful...\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with sitting still and focusing in all aspects of your life, including both work and personal activities?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess it does kind of bleed into everything. I wouldn't call it mood swings. I don't fly off the handle or anything like that, I promise! I just want to be a pleasant person to be around. Is that what you mean? I hope I'm answering that right. I really try not to let things get to me too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your trouble with focusing on tasks is more of a problem for you than your tendency to move around or fidget?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... um, I'm not really sure how to compare them, to be honest. I guess I don't really know which one is... worse? I mean, I really try my best not to let either of them be a bother to anyone! I know I'm always kind of moving around or tapping my feet, and yeah, maybe my mind wanders sometimes when I'm supposed to be focusing, but I really do try so hard to stay on track. \n\nI don't want to say one is a bigger problem than the other because... well, I'm hoping neither of them is too big of a deal, if that makes sense? I just want to do a good job at work and be helpful. Is it okay if I don't really have a clear answer for which one is more of a problem? I hope that's alright... I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble sitting still or focusing in situations outside of work or school?\n\nPatient: Um... outside of work? Well, I guess... I mean, I try really hard to just be relaxed and normal when I'm hanging out with people or, you know, just at home. I don't think it's a huge problem? \n\nLike, if I go to the movies with my friends or something, I might get up to get snacks a few times, or I might kind of bounce my leg, but I always try to make sure I'm not bothering anyone else in the theater! I really don't want to be annoying. And at home, I just like to keep busy, you know? Cleaning up or organizing things. I guess I don't sit down to watch a whole TV show very often, but that's just because I like being productive! \n\nI really try to be respectful in public places, like waiting rooms or whatever. I might pace a little bit if I'm waiting a long time, but I try to stay out of the way. Do you think that sounds okay? I hope I'm not doing anything too weird when I'm out and about. I just want to blend in and be polite, you know?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these difficulties with sitting still and focusing consistently throughout your life?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess it's been happening for a really long time. I don't just... do things without thinking about them first. I always want to make sure I'm following the supervisor's rules and doing exactly what I'm supposed to be doing. \n\nI really hope that's the right answer? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm reckless or a troublemaker or anything like that. I try to be a really good worker. Am I understanding your question okay?\n\nDoctor: When did you first start noticing that you had trouble sitting still or focusing on tasks?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well... I guess it's actually been a really long time. Like, since I was little. I hope you don't judge me for this, because I really did try to be a good student, I promise! But... even way back in elementary school, I guess I was sort of that kid who just couldn't quite stay in her seat. \n\nI didn't mean to be disruptive or anything! I would just get so eager or excited that I'd accidentally talk over the teacher, or I'd get notes sent home to my parents because I'd blurt things out or just kind of... wander around the room when we were supposed to be doing our desk work. I always felt so awful about those notes because I really never wanted to cause trouble. I just had so much energy and it was hard to keep it all in, you know? \n\nIs it normal that it started that far back? I hope I'm explaining this okay... I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had trouble keeping a job or getting along with friends because of your difficulty sitting still or focusing?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess, maybe a little bit? I really try to be the best worker I can be, I really do! But... um, well, I have gotten a few write-ups at work. I felt so bad about them! They were for, um, safety and production things. I didn't mean to mess up, I just get a little ahead of myself sometimes, or I miss a detail when I'm trying to work fast. I'm really worried it might affect my plans to get certified, which I want so badly. \n\nAnd, well, I guess some of my coworkers... they don't always want to partner with me during handoffs. I think maybe I just frustrate them because I'm always moving around or talking a lot, and maybe I miss things they say. I really try to be a good team player, though! I always apologize if I mess up. Do you think they hate working with me? I just want everyone to like me and think I'm doing a good job. Is it okay that I'm struggling with this?\n\nDoctor: Do you have mood swings or get easily upset?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I try really hard to keep a positive attitude, especially at work. I know nobody likes being around someone who's always grumpy or upset, so I really try to just be cheerful and easy to get along with. \n\nI guess sometimes I might get a little frustrated if I'm trying to do something and it's not working out right, but I don't... I wouldn't call it mood swings. I don't fly off the handle or anything like that, I promise! I just want to be a pleasant person to be around. Is that what you mean? I hope I'm answering that right. I really try not to let things get to me too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your trouble with focusing on tasks is more of a problem for you than your tendency to move around or fidget?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... um, I'm not really sure how to compare them, to be honest. I guess I don't really know which one is... worse? I mean, I really try my best not to let either of them be a bother to anyone! I know I'm always kind of moving around or tapping my feet, and yeah, maybe my mind wanders sometimes when I'm supposed to be focusing, but I really do try so hard to stay on track. \n\nI don't want to say one is a bigger problem than the other because... well, I'm hoping neither of them is too big of a deal, if that makes sense? I just want to do a good job at work and be helpful. Is it okay if I don't really have a clear answer for which one is more of a problem? I hope that's alright... I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble sitting still or focusing in situations outside of work or school?\n\nPatient: Um... outside of work? Well, I guess... I mean, I try really hard to just be relaxed and normal when I'm hanging out with people or, you know, just at home. I don't think it's a huge problem? \n\nLike, if I go to the movies with my friends or something, I might get up to get snacks a few times, or I might kind of bounce my leg, but I always try to make sure I'm not bothering anyone else in the theater! I really don't want to be annoying. And at home, I just like to keep busy, you know? Cleaning up or organizing things. I guess I don't sit down to watch a whole TV show very often, but that's just because I like being productive! \n\nI really try to be respectful in public places, like waiting rooms or whatever. I might pace a little bit if I'm waiting a long time, but I try to stay out of the way. Do you think that sounds okay? I hope I'm not doing anything too weird when I'm out and about. I just want to blend in and be polite, you know?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these difficulties with sitting still and focusing consistently throughout your life?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess it's been happening for a really long time. I mean, I don't want to sound like I was a bad kid or a troublemaker or anything! I always really wanted my teachers to like me and be happy with me. But, um... even back in elementary school, I guess I was kind of the kid who couldn't really stay in my seat. \n\nI would accidentally talk over the teacher sometimes... not because I was trying to be rude! I just got so eager to say something, and it would just kind of pop out before I could catch it. And, um, yeah, I did get some notes sent home sometimes because I'd blurt things out or end up wandering around the room when we were supposed to be doing desk work. I really tried to be a good student, though, I promise! I just had a whole lot of extra energy back then. Is it okay that I was like that? I just really hope they knew I wasn't trying to be disrespectful...\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with sitting still and focusing in all aspects of your life, including both work and personal activities?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess it does kind of bleed into everything. I try so hard to keep it all together, especially at work, because I really want to do a good job and be helpful. But... um... I guess it has been a little bit of a problem there lately. \n\nI don't want you to think I'm a bad worker! I really do try my best. But, well, my supervisors have had to talk to me a couple of times... they actually wrote me up, just for some safety and production things. I guess I just get a little too rushed or distracted sometimes. And I'm really worried because I think it might be putting my job and my plans to get my certifications at risk. \n\nPlus, I think some of my coworkers are getting a little frustrated with me. Like, they seem less willing to partner up with me during handoffs, and I hate that because I just want everyone to get along and be a good team. I really am trying to fix it, I promise! Does that make sense? I just hope my boss knows I'm not doing it on purpose... I really want to be a good employee.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel the need to move or fidget when you're at home or in other places outside of work?\n\nPatient: Um... outside of work? Well, I guess... I mean, I try really, really hard to sit still, especially when I'm supposed to be paying attention. I don't want anyone to think I'm not listening! But, um... if I'm in my certification class, or like, if we have a long safety meeting... I guess I do move around a bit. \n\nI don't mean to be a distraction, I promise! But my foot just kind of starts bouncing on its own, or I'll realize I'm clicking my pen a bunch of times, or just... picking at my sleeves. I try to stop when I notice it, but sometimes I'm just shifting in my seat so much that the people next to me will actually ask me to stop. It's so embarrassing when that happens, because I really just want to be respectful and pay attention to whoever is talking. Do you think it's okay that I just have a little trouble staying totally frozen in one spot? I really am trying to be better about it!\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're relaxing or doing quiet activities at home?\n\nPatient: Um... well, quiet activities are a little tough for me, I guess. I try, I really do! But... um... doing quiet stuff kind of makes me feel a little trapped? Like, if I'm on my break at work and I try to just sit in the break room and read the training manual like I'm supposed to... I just can't seem to stay quiet. \n\nI don't mean to bother anyone, but I'll catch myself humming, or tapping my fingers, or I'll just start talking to whoever is in there with me. Or I just end up getting up and walking around instead of actually reading it. I really want to be able to just sit and relax quietly like everyone else does, I promise! I just... I get so antsy. Is it normal to feel like that when you're just trying to have some downtime? I hope I don't sound like I'm just making excuses...\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Um... oh gosh. I really, really try not to do that, because I know it's not polite. But... I guess sometimes I do kind of cut in on people without meaning to. \n\nI don't mean to be rude or anything, I promise! It's just... like, if my coworkers are having a conversation, I might just sort of jump right in before they're finished. Or, um, if the shift lead is asking a question, I'll catch myself blurting out the answer before they're even done talking. \n\nI just get so eager to help or join in, but then... well, everybody gets a little irritated with me. And I hate that, because I really want them to like me and I want to be respectful. It's just like the words come out before I can stop them, you know? Do you think people understand that I'm not trying to be disrespectful? I really am trying so hard to get better at waiting my turn to speak...\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty with staying focused or your tendency to fidget and move around is more of a problem for you?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess they're both kind of a problem? I mean, I really try to keep it together, I promise I do! But... um... it's just that at work, it's starting to cause some issues, and I feel so terrible about it. \n\nI really want to get my certification and do a good job, but... my supervisors have actually written me up a couple of times. Just for, like, some safety and production stuff. I didn't mean to mess up, I just get distracted or I'm moving too fast! And... well, some of my coworkers don't really want to partner with me as much during handoffs anymore. \n\nIt makes me so sad because I really want to be a good team player and I want them to trust me. I'm trying so hard to fix it, I really am! Do you think I can still turn things around at work if I keep trying my best to focus more? I just don't want them to think I'm a bad worker...\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would have trouble sitting still and focusing if you were doing an activity that you really enjoyed?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, well... I guess it depends on what it is? If it's something I really, really love doing, I can usually stay with it a little better. But... um... if it's something I have to do for work, even if I know it's super important, I still kind of struggle. \n\nLike, when I'm doing the inventory logs or... or reading the CNC training pages? I promise I start out really trying my best to pay attention! I sit down and I'm like, \"Okay, I'm going to read this and understand it.\" But then... well, after just a few minutes, it's like the numbers or the words just start to blur together. I end up having to go back and read the same thing over and over because I realize I didn't actually take any of it in. \n\nI don't mean to be careless with it, I really don't! It's just so hard to keep my brain locked onto it. Does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm just not trying hard enough, because I really am giving it my all...\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulties with focusing and paying attention are more problematic for you than your tendency to fidget and be restless?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I guess maybe the restless part is actually a really big problem too? I mean, I try so hard to just sit quietly, I promise I do. But... well, if I have to sit for the certification class, or, um, like a long safety meeting at the plant? I just can't seem to stay still. \n\nI'll catch myself bouncing my foot a million miles an hour, or I'm clicking my pen over and over, or picking at my sleeves. I don't even realize I'm doing it half the time! But then I end up shifting around in my seat so much that... well, sometimes people actually ask me to stop. \n\nIt's just so embarrassing because I really don't want to be a distraction to anyone else. I want to be respectful of the instructors and my coworkers, you know? I feel awful when I bother them. Do you think they know I'm not doing it on purpose? I really am trying my best to just sit normally like everyone else, I just have so much nervous energy...\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Um... well... honestly, I think it's always been kind of like this? I really, really tried to be a good student back then, I promise I did! But... um... even in elementary school, I was sort of the kid who just couldn't stay in my seat.
